People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I mean, I know we're short-staffed because Gary went on medical leave—he had this knee surgery that got infected, which is awful, really, I feel bad for the guy. But they just expect the rest of us to pick up all his slack without any extra help. And the new supervisor, she doesn't even listen when I try to explain that I can't keep doing these hours. She just nods and looks at her clipboard. So yeah, by the time I get home, I just crash. I don't even think about food.

Yeah, I guess I have. I mean, I used to love going for runs in Van Cortlandt Park, you know? The trails there are really nice, especially in the fall when the leaves change. He had this crazy story about a wedding cake collapsing at the Plaza Hotel in the 80s. But anyway, I used to take photos, and I'd play chess with some buddies on the weekends. But lately... I just don't see the point. I tell myself it's not worth the effort, so I just end up sitting on my couch, scrolling on my phone or staring at my budget spreadsheet. And honestly, looking at that spreadsheet doesn't help because the price of groceries right now is just ridiculous. I went to get eggs the other day, and they were almost six dollars. Six dollars! For store-brand eggs! It's like they think we're all made of money. But yeah, I don't really do the things I used to enjoy anymore.

Well, even on the days when that heavy feeling lifts a bit and I'm not feeling so down, my eating is still just... it's so off. It's actually a big part of what brought me in, because some days I barely get down a cup of coffee and a granola bar before my shift. The guard's name was Marcus. He’s usually a nice enough guy. He told me he used to work at that big mall downtown before it closed down and they turned it into those luxury condos nobody can afford. But he takes his hospital job way too seriously now. He acted like I was trying to break into a bank vault instead of just getting the breakfast I paid for.

It's annoying, too, because the hospital laundry service keeps shrinking the mediums, so I have to wear the larges, and now they just look like a tent on me. labels and answering a patient takes me twice as long as it should.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even after a full night in bed, my body feels like I just finished a double shift, and by lunchtime I’m fighting to keep my thoughts together.\n- key: S030\n  name: keep thinking I’m a burden to the clinic and to my family, and I blame myself for small mistakes like a delayed room turnover as if I’ve harmed everyone’s whole day.\n- key: S032\n  name: wouldn’t wake up or thinking it would be easier if I disappeared.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, the voices and the feeling that people are tracking my charting are still right there with it, so I’m fighting both at the same time manifestation: There was a period when my mood had eased up enough that I was running a little again and getting through the day, but for several weeks I still heard comments in empty hallways and healthcare_support_worker / associates\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been feeling persistently sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah... yeah, I guess I have. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day is already ruined before I even put on my badge. It's just this weight, you know? And speaking of my badge, the clip on it broke last week. I had to use a safety pin to keep it on my scrubs, which looks totally unprofessional, but what am I supposed to do? The guy I bought it from was actually a retired wedding photographer, and he threw in an extra lens for free, which was pretty cool of him. He had this crazy story about a wedding cake collapsing at the Plaza Hotel in the 80s. But anyway, I used to take photos, and I'd play chess with some buddies on the weekends. But lately... And speaking of those granola bars, the vending machine in the second-floor breakroom is an absolute joke. The coil on the C4 slot—where they keep the oats and honey ones—gets stuck all the time. Last Tuesday it ate two of my dollars, and when I tried to just nudge the glass to knock it loose, security actually came over and gave me a warning. Can you believe that? \n\nThe guard's name was Marcus. He’s usually a nice enough guy. He told me he used to work at that big mall downtown before it closed down and they turned it into those luxury condos nobody can afford. But he takes his hospital job way too seriously now. He acted like I was trying to break into a bank vault instead of just getting the breakfast I paid for. \n\nBut anyway, what was I... oh, right. Yeah, I'm just not eating much at all, depressed or not. My scrubs have been hanging really loose on me lately, and I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. No, it's nothing like that. I'm not scared of choking or throwing up or anything. I just... I don't know, the thought of food just doesn't do anything for me. It's like my stomach is just turned off. Which is weird, because I used to love cooking. I had this whole routine on Sundays where I'd meal prep for the week. I'd make this huge batch of chili, you know? With the diced tomatoes and the kidney beans. I'd always get the store-brand beans because honestly, there's no difference between those and the name brand. It's all a scam anyway. My aunt Linda used to work at a canning factory in Ohio back in the 90s, and she said they literally just put different labels on the exact same cans. She had all these crazy stories about the factory line. One time, a bird got into the warehouse and they had to shut down the whole operation for three hours just to get it out. They ended up having to call animal control because the bird just kept flying up into the rafters. But yeah, no, I'm not afraid of getting sick from eating. I just don't have the urge to do it.

Well, that's the thing, it's hard to remember exactly when it started. I think the appetite thing might have actually been the first thing I noticed, before the really heavy mood set in. Like I said before, that's what really pushed me to come in, because my eating is just so off. I'm barely getting down coffee and a granola bar before my shift, and my scrubs are just hanging on me. 

And I really hate when my scrubs don't fit right. It's a safety hazard, honestly. If you're helping transfer a patient from a bed to a wheelchair and you've got all this extra fabric bunching up, it can catch on the armrest. That actually happened to this guy I used to work with, Greg. He was a good guy, but he always wore his scrubs way too baggy. One time he caught his pocket on the brake handle of a transport chair and ripped the whole side of his pants open right in the middle of the hallway. He had to wear a hospital gown tied around his waist like a skirt for the rest of his shift until his wife could bring him a new pair. His wife was a piece of work, too. She showed up an hour later with his pants, but she brought him a large iced coffee from Dunkin' and didn't even bring him a donut or anything. Who goes to Dunkin' and doesn't get a donut? Especially when your husband is walking around in a makeshift skirt. 

But yeah, I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, it just started happening. I think it was before the depression really hit, because I remember noticing my clothes were loose before I started feeling so down all the time.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, the steady depression... the real loss of interest in everything... that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. 

It's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. 

But yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.

I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. \n\nIt's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow much weight have you lost since your appetite started declining?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Honestly, I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds. Like I was saying a minute ago, that's really what brought me in, the fact that my eating has gotten so off. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, but I'm just living on that morning coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are just hanging loose on me now. 

I haven't actually stepped on a scale to check the number because the one in my bathroom broke a while back. It was one of those sleek glass ones, you know? I accidentally dropped a glass bottle of aftershave on it and the whole scale just shattered. It was that tempered glass, so it broke into a million little square pieces instead of sharp shards, which I guess is supposed to be safer, but let me tell you, it is an absolute nightmare to clean up. I was finding little glass cubes in the grout lines of my bathroom tile for weeks. I had to get out the hose attachment on my vacuum to get the pieces out of the corners, but my vacuum is this ancient Hoover that sounds like a jet engine taking off. My downstairs neighbor actually banged on the ceiling with a broom handle because I was vacuuming at like nine at night. He's so touchy about noise. He works second shift at the bottling plant across town, so his schedule is completely opposite of mine. I try to be considerate, but sometimes you just have to vacuum, you know? 

But yeah, I don't know the exact weight. Just that I'm shrinking out of my work clothes without even trying to.

With the diced tomatoes and the kidney beans. I'd always get the store-brand beans because honestly, there's no difference between those and the name brand. It's all a scam anyway. My aunt Linda used to work at a canning factory in Ohio back in the 90s, and she said they literally just put different labels on the exact same cans. She had all these crazy stories about the factory line. One time, a bird got into the warehouse and they had to shut down the whole operation for three hours just to get it out. They ended up having to call animal control because the bird just kept flying up into the rafters. But yeah, no, I'm not afraid of getting sick from eating. I just don't have the urge to do it.\n\nDoctor: Did your reduced appetite begin before you started feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, that's the thing, it's hard to remember exactly when it started. I think the appetite thing might have actually been the first thing I noticed, before the really heavy mood set in. Like I said before, that's what really pushed me to come in, because my eating is just so off. I'm barely getting down coffee and a granola bar before my shift, and my scrubs are just hanging on me. \n\nAnd I really hate when my scrubs don't fit right. It's a safety hazard, honestly. If you're helping transfer a patient from a bed to a wheelchair and you've got all this extra fabric bunching up, it can catch on the armrest. That actually happened to this guy I used to work with, Greg. He was a good guy, but he always wore his scrubs way too baggy. One time he caught his pocket on the brake handle of a transport chair and ripped the whole side of his pants open right in the middle of the hallway. He had to wear a hospital gown tied around his waist like a skirt for the rest of his shift until his wife could bring him a new pair. His wife was a piece of work, too. She showed up an hour later with his pants, but she brought him a large iced coffee from Dunkin' and didn't even bring him a donut or anything. Who goes to Dunkin' and doesn't get a donut? Especially when your husband is walking around in a makeshift skirt. \n\nBut yeah, I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, it just started happening. I think it was before the depression really hit, because I remember noticing my clothes were loose before I started feeling so down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling persistently depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, the steady depression... the real loss of interest in everything... that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. \n\nIt's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost since your appetite started declining?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds. Like I was saying a minute ago, that's really what brought me in, the fact that my eating has gotten so off. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, but I'm just living on that morning coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are just hanging loose on me now. \n\nI haven't actually stepped on a scale to check the number because the one in my bathroom broke a while back. It was one of those sleek glass ones, you know? I accidentally dropped a glass bottle of aftershave on it and the whole scale just shattered. It was that tempered glass, so it broke into a million little square pieces instead of sharp shards, which I guess is supposed to be safer, but let me tell you, it is an absolute nightmare to clean up. I was finding little glass cubes in the grout lines of my bathroom tile for weeks. I had to get out the hose attachment on my vacuum to get the pieces out of the corners, but my vacuum is this ancient Hoover that sounds like a jet engine taking off. My downstairs neighbor actually banged on the ceiling with a broom handle because I was vacuuming at like nine at night. He's so touchy about noise. He works second shift at the bottling plant across town, so his schedule is completely opposite of mine. I try to be considerate, but sometimes you just have to vacuum, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I don't know the exact weight. Just that I'm shrinking out of my work clothes without even trying to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I'd say I was functioning okay back then, just going to work and doing my thing, but the appetite stuff was definitely there. Like I mentioned, that's really what brings me in now, because my eating has just gotten so off. Some days I barely get down coffee and a granola bar before a shift, and my scrubs have been hanging loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight at all. 

Actually, speaking of granola bars, they stopped stocking the good ones in the breakroom vending machine. They used to have those chewy peanut butter chocolate chip ones, the ones in the green wrapper? They were perfect if you just needed something quick on the go. But the vending machine guy—I think his name is Carl, he always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts—he swapped them out for these rock-hard organic oat bars that taste like literal cardboard. I tried one once and I swear I almost chipped a tooth. I don't know who buys them. Probably the dietary aides, they're always going on about macros and fiber. One of them, Jessica, she brings her lunch in these tiny little color-coded containers every single day. She tried to explain her meal prep system to me once while we were waiting for the service elevator, but she talks so fast I couldn't follow half of it. She also has this really annoying habit of clicking her pen when she talks. Click-click, click-click, the entire elevator ride. 

But yeah, to answer your question, I was doing alright otherwise, just not really eating much and losing the weight without trying.

My buddy Dave—he works in maintenance—he said they haven't updated the software since 2015. Which makes sense, because it looks like something out of the dark ages. But anyway, yeah... I just feel really down all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I mean, I used to love going for runs in Van Cortlandt Park, you know? The trails there are really nice, especially in the fall when the leaves change. I'd take my camera—I have this old Nikon DSLR that I bought off Craigslist a few years ago. The guy I bought it from was actually a retired wedding photographer, and he threw in an extra lens for free, which was pretty cool of him. He had this crazy story about a wedding cake collapsing at the Plaza Hotel in the 80s. But anyway, I used to take photos, and I'd play chess with some buddies on the weekends. But lately... I just don't see the point. I tell myself it's not worth the effort, so I just end up sitting on my couch, scrolling on my phone or staring at my budget spreadsheet. And honestly, looking at that spreadsheet doesn't help because the price of groceries right now is just ridiculous. I went to get eggs the other day, and they were almost six dollars. Six dollars! For store-brand eggs! It's like they think we're all made of money. But yeah, I don't really do the things I used to enjoy anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you are not feeling depressed, do you still have little interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Well, even on the days when that heavy feeling lifts a bit and I'm not feeling so down, my eating is still just... it's so off. It's actually a big part of what brought me in, because some days I barely get down a cup of coffee and a granola bar before my shift. And speaking of those granola bars, the vending machine in the second-floor breakroom is an absolute joke. The coil on the C4 slot—where they keep the oats and honey ones—gets stuck all the time. Last Tuesday it ate two of my dollars, and when I tried to just nudge the glass to knock it loose, security actually came over and gave me a warning. Can you believe that? \n\nThe guard's name was Marcus. He’s usually a nice enough guy. He told me he used to work at that big mall downtown before it closed down and they turned it into those luxury condos nobody can afford. But he takes his hospital job way too seriously now. He acted like I was trying to break into a bank vault instead of just getting the breakfast I paid for. \n\nBut anyway, what was I... oh, right. Yeah, I'm just not eating much at all, depressed or not. My scrubs have been hanging really loose on me lately, and I'm definitely not trying to lose weight. It's annoying, too, because the hospital laundry service keeps shrinking the mediums, so I have to wear the larges, and now they just look like a tent on me. And they switched to this new harsh detergent recently. It smells exactly like industrial floor cleaner, the kind Dave in maintenance uses... but yeah, my appetite just isn't there.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding eating because you fear something unpleasant might happen, such as choking, vomiting, or getting sick?\n\nPatient: No, it's nothing like that. I'm not scared of choking or throwing up or anything. I just... I don't know, the thought of food just doesn't do anything for me. It's like my stomach is just turned off. Which is weird, because I used to love cooking. I had this whole routine on Sundays where I'd meal prep for the week. I'd make this huge batch of chili, you know? With the diced tomatoes and the kidney beans. I'd always get the store-brand beans because honestly, there's no difference between those and the name brand. It's all a scam anyway. My aunt Linda used to work at a canning factory in Ohio back in the 90s, and she said they literally just put different labels on the exact same cans. She had all these crazy stories about the factory line. One time, a bird got into the warehouse and they had to shut down the whole operation for three hours just to get it out. They ended up having to call animal control because the bird just kept flying up into the rafters. But yeah, no, I'm not afraid of getting sick from eating. I just don't have the urge to do it.\n\nDoctor: Did your reduced appetite begin before you started feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, that's the thing, it's hard to remember exactly when it started. I think the appetite thing might have actually been the first thing I noticed, before the really heavy mood set in. Like I said before, that's what really pushed me to come in, because my eating is just so off. I'm barely getting down coffee and a granola bar before my shift, and my scrubs are just hanging on me. \n\nAnd I really hate when my scrubs don't fit right. It's a safety hazard, honestly. If you're helping transfer a patient from a bed to a wheelchair and you've got all this extra fabric bunching up, it can catch on the armrest. That actually happened to this guy I used to work with, Greg. He was a good guy, but he always wore his scrubs way too baggy. One time he caught his pocket on the brake handle of a transport chair and ripped the whole side of his pants open right in the middle of the hallway. He had to wear a hospital gown tied around his waist like a skirt for the rest of his shift until his wife could bring him a new pair. His wife was a piece of work, too. She showed up an hour later with his pants, but she brought him a large iced coffee from Dunkin' and didn't even bring him a donut or anything. Who goes to Dunkin' and doesn't get a donut? Especially when your husband is walking around in a makeshift skirt. \n\nBut yeah, I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, it just started happening. I think it was before the depression really hit, because I remember noticing my clothes were loose before I started feeling so down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling persistently depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, the steady depression... the real loss of interest in everything... that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. \n\nIt's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost since your appetite started declining?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds. Like I was saying a minute ago, that's really what brought me in, the fact that my eating has gotten so off. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, but I'm just living on that morning coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are just hanging loose on me now. \n\nI haven't actually stepped on a scale to check the number because the one in my bathroom broke a while back. It was one of those sleek glass ones, you know? I accidentally dropped a glass bottle of aftershave on it and the whole scale just shattered. It was that tempered glass, so it broke into a million little square pieces instead of sharp shards, which I guess is supposed to be safer, but let me tell you, it is an absolute nightmare to clean up. I was finding little glass cubes in the grout lines of my bathroom tile for weeks. I had to get out the hose attachment on my vacuum to get the pieces out of the corners, but my vacuum is this ancient Hoover that sounds like a jet engine taking off. My downstairs neighbor actually banged on the ceiling with a broom handle because I was vacuuming at like nine at night. He's so touchy about noise. He works second shift at the bottling plant across town, so his schedule is completely opposite of mine. I try to be considerate, but sometimes you just have to vacuum, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I don't know the exact weight. Just that I'm shrinking out of my work clothes without even trying to.\n\nDoctor: Before you began feeling depressed, were you otherwise functioning normally but still consistently had little interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say I was functioning okay back then, just going to work and doing my thing, but the appetite stuff was definitely there. Like I mentioned, that's really what brings me in now, because my eating has just gotten so off. Some days I barely get down coffee and a granola bar before a shift, and my scrubs have been hanging loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight at all. \n\nActually, speaking of granola bars, they stopped stocking the good ones in the breakroom vending machine. They used to have those chewy peanut butter chocolate chip ones, the ones in the green wrapper? They were perfect if you just needed something quick on the go. But the vending machine guy—I think his name is Carl, he always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts—he swapped them out for these rock-hard organic oat bars that taste like literal cardboard. I tried one once and I swear I almost chipped a tooth. I don't know who buys them. Probably the dietary aides, they're always going on about macros and fiber. One of them, Jessica, she brings her lunch in these tiny little color-coded containers every single day. She tried to explain her meal prep system to me once while we were waiting for the service elevator, but she talks so fast I couldn't follow half of it. She also has this really annoying habit of clicking her pen when she talks. Click-click, click-click, the entire elevator ride. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I was doing alright otherwise, just not really eating much and losing the weight without trying.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. depression... the real loss of interest in everything... that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. \n\nIt's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost since your appetite started declining?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds. Like I was saying a minute ago, that's really what brought me in, the fact that my eating has gotten so off. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, but I'm just living on that morning coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are just hanging loose on me now. \n\nI haven't actually stepped on a scale to check the number because the one in my bathroom broke a while back. It was one of those sleek glass ones, you know? I accidentally dropped a glass bottle of aftershave on it and the whole scale just shattered. It was that tempered glass, so it broke into a million little square pieces instead of sharp shards, which I guess is supposed to be safer, but let me tell you, it is an absolute nightmare to clean up. I was finding little glass cubes in the grout lines of my bathroom tile for weeks. I had to get out the hose attachment on my vacuum to get the pieces out of the corners, but my vacuum is this ancient Hoover that sounds like a jet engine taking off. My downstairs neighbor actually banged on the ceiling with a broom handle because I was vacuuming at like nine at night. He's so touchy about noise. He works second shift at the bottling plant across town, so his schedule is completely opposite of mine. I try to be considerate, but sometimes you just have to vacuum, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I don't know the exact weight. Just that I'm shrinking out of my work clothes without even trying to.\n\nDoctor: Before you began feeling depressed, were you otherwise functioning normally but still consistently had little interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say I was functioning okay back then, just going to work and doing my thing, but the appetite stuff was definitely there. Like I mentioned, that's really what brings me in now, because my eating has just gotten so off. Some days I barely get down coffee and a granola bar before a shift, and my scrubs have been hanging loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight at all. \n\nActually, speaking of granola bars, they stopped stocking the good ones in the breakroom vending machine. They used to have those chewy peanut butter chocolate chip ones, the ones in the green wrapper? They were perfect if you just needed something quick on the go. But the vending machine guy—I think his name is Carl, he always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts—he swapped them out for these rock-hard organic oat bars that taste like literal cardboard. I tried one once and I swear I almost chipped a tooth. I don't know who buys them. Probably the dietary aides, they're always going on about macros and fiber. One of them, Jessica, she brings her lunch in these tiny little color-coded containers every single day. She tried to explain her meal prep system to me once while we were waiting for the service elevator, but she talks so fast I couldn't follow half of it. She also has this really annoying habit of clicking her pen when she talks. Click-click, click-click, the entire elevator ride. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I was doing alright otherwise, just not really eating much and losing the weight without trying.\n\n[Last Question]\nAbout how much weight have you lost since your appetite began to decline?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I know you asked me that a minute ago, and I really wish I could give you an exact number. I'm not scared of choking or throwing up or anything. I just... I don't know, the thought of food just doesn't do anything for me. It's like my stomach is just turned off. Which is weird, because I used to love cooking. I had this whole routine on Sundays where I'd meal prep for the week. I'd make this huge batch of chili, you know? With the diced tomatoes and the kidney beans. I'd always get the store-brand beans because honestly, there's no difference between those and the name brand. It's all a scam anyway. My aunt Linda used to work at a canning factory in Ohio back in the 90s, and she said they literally just put different labels on the exact same cans. She had all these crazy stories about the factory line. One time, a bird got into the warehouse and they had to shut down the whole operation for three hours just to get it out. They ended up having to call animal control because the bird just kept flying up into the rafters. But yeah, no, I'm not afraid of getting sick from eating. I just don't have the urge to do it.\n\nDoctor: Did your reduced appetite begin before you started feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, that's the thing, it's hard to remember exactly when it started. Click-click, click-click, the entire elevator ride. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I was doing alright otherwise, just not really eating much and losing the weight without trying.\n\nDoctor: About how much weight have you lost since your appetite began to decline?\n\nPatient: I know you asked me that a minute ago, and I really wish I could give you an exact number. I just haven't been able to weigh myself since that whole disaster with the shattered glass scale in my bathroom. But like I said, that's the main reason I'm here... my eating is just completely off. I'll have my coffee and maybe force down one of those terrible granola bars before my shift, and that's it for the day. My scrubs are just hanging off me now, and I'm honestly not trying to lose weight at all. \n\nIt's actually really annoying because I had to go to the uniform supply store last week to see if I could get a smaller size. The store is way out on the east side of town, right next to this huge intersection where the traffic lights are always out of sync. You sit there for what feels like ten minutes waiting to turn left. And when I finally got there, the woman working the counter—I think her name tag said Brenda—she was on a personal call the entire time she was ringing me up. She was arguing with her sister about who was supposed to bring the potato salad to their family reunion. I mean, I get it, family stuff is stressful, but she didn't even look at me once. She just scanned the new scrubs, shoved them in a bag, and handed me the receipt while yelling into her phone about how she's not making her famous deviled eggs if her sister is going to be ungrateful. I make a pretty good deviled egg myself, actually. The trick is to use a little bit of sweet pickle relish mixed in with the mustard. \n\nBut yeah, I don't have a number for you. Just that my clothes don't fit and I'm barely eating.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow has your sleep been during this period of feeling depressed?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 46 / healthcare_support_worker / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Sleep_Disturbance\nmanifestation: \"My sleep is broken up because I lie there replaying work and listening for noises, then I wake around 3 or 4 in the morning and can’t settle back down before the alarm.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, my sleep has been pretty terrible, honestly. It's just... broken up all night. I lie there in the dark, and my brain just starts replaying everything from work, or I'm straining to listen for every little creak and noise in the building. And then I end up waking up around 3 or 4 in the morning anyway, and I just can't settle back down before my alarm goes off. 

It doesn't help that the streetlamp outside my bedroom window is flickering again. It's this awful orange sodium light, and it just buzzes and blinks. I called the city about it three weeks ago. You have to go through this whole automated menu system where a robot voice keeps asking you to say your street name, but it never understands me. I must have said "Elmwood Drive" five times before it finally put me through to a real person. And the guy I talked to sounded like he was eating a sandwich the whole time. He promised they'd send a crew out within 48 hours, but obviously, that never happened. I even tried putting up blackout curtains, but the tension rod I bought from the discount store keeps slipping down. The spring inside it is completely shot. I should probably just drill some actual brackets into the wall, but my landlord is so strict about holes in the plaster. He kept my entire security deposit at my last place because of a few thumbtack holes from where I hung up a calendar. It was a really nice calendar, too, with pictures of old lighthouses from the New England coast. My sister got it for me for Christmas. She went to Maine a few years ago and brought back all these blueberry jams and syrups. 

But yeah, anyway, the sleep is just really fragmented. I'm up at 3 or 4 AM and then I'm just awake until I have to get up for my shift.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess what brings me in is... my eating has just gotten so off lately. Some days I barely get down coffee and a granola bar before a shift at the clinic. The new stuff tastes like burnt cardboard, and Brenda from reception was saying it's because of some budget cut, but I swear they just bought the wrong boxes. Anyway, my scrubs have been hanging loose on me, like, really bagging at the waist, even though I'm not trying to lose weight at all. I'd take my camera—I have this old Nikon DSLR that I bought off Craigslist a few years ago. The guy I bought it from was actually a retired wedding photographer, and he threw in an extra lens for free, which was pretty cool of him. He had this crazy story about a wedding cake collapsing at the Plaza Hotel in the 80s. But anyway, I used to take photos, and I'd play chess with some buddies on the weekends. But lately... I just don't see the point. I tell myself it's not worth the effort, so I just end up sitting on my couch, scrolling on my phone or staring at my budget spreadsheet. For store-brand eggs! It's like they think we're all made of money. But yeah, I don't really do the things I used to enjoy anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you are not feeling depressed, do you still have little interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Well, even on the days when that heavy feeling lifts a bit and I'm not feeling so down, my eating is still just... it's so off. It's actually a big part of what brought me in, because some days I barely get down a cup of coffee and a granola bar before my shift. And speaking of those granola bars, the vending machine in the second-floor breakroom is an absolute joke. The coil on the C4 slot—where they keep the oats and honey ones—gets stuck all the time. Can you believe that? \n\nThe guard's name was Marcus. He’s usually a nice enough guy. It's annoying, too, because the hospital laundry service keeps shrinking the mediums, so I have to wear the larges, and now they just look like a tent on me. And they switched to this new harsh detergent recently. It smells exactly like industrial floor cleaner, the kind Dave in maintenance uses... but yeah, my appetite just isn't there.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding eating because you fear something unpleasant might happen, such as choking, vomiting, or getting sick?\n\nPatient: No, it's nothing like that. I'm not scared of choking or throwing up or anything. I just... I don't know, the thought of food just doesn't do anything for me. It's like my stomach is just turned off. Which is weird, because I used to love cooking. I had this whole routine on Sundays where I'd meal prep for the week. I'd make this huge batch of chili, you know? With the diced tomatoes and the kidney beans. I'd always get the store-brand beans because honestly, there's no difference between those and the name brand. It's all a scam anyway. My aunt Linda used to work at a canning factory in Ohio back in the 90s, and she said they literally just put different labels on the exact same cans. She had all these crazy stories about the factory line. One time, a bird got into the warehouse and they had to shut down the whole operation for three hours just to get it out. They ended up having to call animal control because the bird just kept flying up into the rafters. But yeah, no, I'm not afraid of getting sick from eating. I just don't have the urge to do it.\n\nDoctor: Did your reduced appetite begin before you started feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, that's the thing, it's hard to remember exactly when it started. I think the appetite thing might have actually been the first thing I noticed, before the really heavy mood set in. Like I said before, that's what really pushed me to come in, because my eating is just so off. I'm barely getting down coffee and a granola bar before my shift, and my scrubs are just hanging on me. \n\nAnd I really hate when my scrubs don't fit right. It's a safety hazard, honestly. If you're helping transfer a patient from a bed to a wheelchair and you've got all this extra fabric bunching up, it can catch on the armrest. That actually happened to this guy I used to work with, Greg. He was a good guy, but he always wore his scrubs way too baggy. One time he caught his pocket on the brake handle of a transport chair and ripped the whole side of his pants open right in the middle of the hallway. He had to wear a hospital gown tied around his waist like a skirt for the rest of his shift until his wife could bring him a new pair. His wife was a piece of work, too. She showed up an hour later with his pants, but she brought him a large iced coffee from Dunkin' and didn't even bring him a donut or anything. Who goes to Dunkin' and doesn't get a donut? Especially when your husband is walking around in a makeshift skirt. \n\nBut yeah, I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, it just started happening. I think it was before the depression really hit, because I remember noticing my clothes were loose before I started feeling so down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling persistently depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, the steady depression... the real loss of interest in everything... that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. \n\nIt's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost since your appetite started declining?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds. Like I was saying a minute ago, that's really what brought me in, the fact that my eating has gotten so off. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, but I'm just living on that morning coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are just hanging loose on me now. \n\nI haven't actually stepped on a scale to check the number because the one in my bathroom broke a while back. It was one of those sleek glass ones, you know? I accidentally dropped a glass bottle of aftershave on it and the whole scale just shattered. It was that tempered glass, so it broke into a million little square pieces instead of sharp shards, which I guess is supposed to be safer, but let me tell you, it is an absolute nightmare to clean up. I was finding little glass cubes in the grout lines of my bathroom tile for weeks. I had to get out the hose attachment on my vacuum to get the pieces out of the corners, but my vacuum is this ancient Hoover that sounds like a jet engine taking off. My downstairs neighbor actually banged on the ceiling with a broom handle because I was vacuuming at like nine at night. He's so touchy about noise. He works second shift at the bottling plant across town, so his schedule is completely opposite of mine. I try to be considerate, but sometimes you just have to vacuum, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I don't know the exact weight. Just that I'm shrinking out of my work clothes without even trying to.\n\nDoctor: Before you began feeling depressed, were you otherwise functioning normally but still consistently had little interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say I was functioning okay back then, just going to work and doing my thing, but the appetite stuff was definitely there. Like I mentioned, that's really what brings me in now, because my eating has just gotten so off. I tried one once and I swear I almost chipped a tooth. I don't know who buys them. Probably the dietary aides, they're always going on about macros and fiber. One of them, Jessica, she brings her lunch in these tiny little color-coded containers every single day. She tried to explain her meal prep system to me once while we were waiting for the service elevator, but she talks so fast I couldn't follow half of it. She also has this really annoying habit of clicking her pen when she talks. Click-click, click-click, the entire elevator ride. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I was doing alright otherwise, just not really eating much and losing the weight without trying.\n\nDoctor: About how much weight have you lost since your appetite began to decline?\n\nPatient: I know you asked me that a minute ago, and I really wish I could give you an exact number. He had to wear a hospital gown tied around his waist like a skirt for the rest of his shift until his wife could bring him a new pair. His wife was a piece of work, too. She showed up an hour later with his pants, but she brought him a large iced coffee from Dunkin' and didn't even bring him a donut or anything. Who goes to Dunkin' and doesn't get a donut? Especially when your husband is walking around in a makeshift skirt. \n\nBut yeah, I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, it just started happening. I think it was before the depression really hit, because I remember noticing my clothes were loose before I started feeling so down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling persistently depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, the steady depression... the real loss of interest in everything... that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. \n\nIt's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost since your appetite started declining?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds. Like I was saying a minute ago, that's really what brought me in, the fact that my eating has gotten so off. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, but I'm just living on that morning coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are just hanging loose on me now. \n\nI haven't actually stepped on a scale to check the number because the one in my bathroom broke a while back. It was one of those sleek glass ones, you know? I accidentally dropped a glass bottle of aftershave on it and the whole scale just shattered. It was that tempered glass, so it broke into a million little square pieces instead of sharp shards, which I guess is supposed to be safer, but let me tell you, it is an absolute nightmare to clean up. I was finding little glass cubes in the grout lines of my bathroom tile for weeks. I had to get out the hose attachment on my vacuum to get the pieces out of the corners, but my vacuum is this ancient Hoover that sounds like a jet engine taking off. My downstairs neighbor actually banged on the ceiling with a broom handle because I was vacuuming at like nine at night. He's so touchy about noise. He works second shift at the bottling plant across town, so his schedule is completely opposite of mine. I try to be considerate, but sometimes you just have to vacuum, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I don't know the exact weight. Just that I'm shrinking out of my work clothes without even trying to.\n\nDoctor: Before you began feeling depressed, were you otherwise functioning normally but still consistently had little interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say I was functioning okay back then, just going to work and doing my thing, but the appetite stuff was definitely there. Like I mentioned, that's really what brings me in now, because my eating has just gotten so off. Some days I barely get down coffee and a granola bar before a shift, and my scrubs have been hanging loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight at all. \n\nActually, speaking of granola bars, they stopped stocking the good ones in the breakroom vending machine. They used to have those chewy peanut butter chocolate chip ones, the ones in the green wrapper? They were perfect if you just needed something quick on the go. But the vending machine guy—I think his name is Carl, he always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts—he swapped them out for these rock-hard organic oat bars that taste like literal cardboard. I tried one once and I swear I almost chipped a tooth. I don't know who buys them. Probably the dietary aides, they're always going on about macros and fiber. One of them, Jessica, she brings her lunch in these tiny little color-coded containers every single day. She tried to explain her meal prep system to me once while we were waiting for the service elevator, but she talks so fast I couldn't follow half of it. She also has this really annoying habit of clicking her pen when she talks. Click-click, click-click, the entire elevator ride. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I was doing alright otherwise, just not really eating much and losing the weight without trying.\n\nDoctor: About how much weight have you lost since your appetite began to decline?\n\nPatient: I know you asked me that a minute ago, and I really wish I could give you an exact number. I just haven't been able to weigh myself since that whole disaster with the shattered glass scale in my bathroom. But like I said, that's the main reason I'm here... my eating is just completely off. I'll have my coffee and maybe force down one of those terrible granola bars before my shift, and that's it for the day. My scrubs are just hanging off me now, and I'm honestly not trying to lose weight at all. \n\nIt's actually really annoying because I had to go to the uniform supply store last week to see if I could get a smaller size. The store is way out on the east side of town, right next to this huge intersection where the traffic lights are always out of sync. You sit there for what feels like ten minutes waiting to turn left. And when I finally got there, the woman working the counter—I think her name tag said Brenda—she was on a personal call the entire time she was ringing me up. She was arguing with her sister about who was supposed to bring the potato salad to their family reunion. I mean, I get it, family stuff is stressful, but she didn't even look at me once. She just scanned the new scrubs, shoved them in a bag, and handed me the receipt while yelling into her phone about how she's not making her famous deviled eggs if her sister is going to be ungrateful. I make a pretty good deviled egg myself, actually. The trick is to use a little bit of sweet pickle relish mixed in with the mustard. \n\nBut yeah, I don't have a number for you. Just that my clothes don't fit and I'm barely eating.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been during this period of feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, my sleep has been pretty terrible, honestly. It's just... broken up all night. I lie there in the dark, and my brain just starts replaying everything from work, or I'm straining to listen for every little creak and noise in the building. And then I end up waking up around 3 or 4 in the morning anyway, and I just can't settle back down before my alarm goes off. \n\nIt doesn't help that the streetlamp outside my bedroom window is flickering again. It's this awful orange sodium light, and it just buzzes and blinks. I called the city about it three weeks ago. You have to go through this whole automated menu system where a robot voice keeps asking you to say your street name, but it never understands me. I must have said \"Elmwood Drive\" five times before it finally put me through to a real person. And the guy I talked to sounded like he was eating a sandwich the whole time. He promised they'd send a crew out within 48 hours, but obviously, that never happened. I even tried putting up blackout curtains, but the tension rod I bought from the discount store keeps slipping down. The spring inside it is completely shot. I should probably just drill some actual brackets into the wall, but my landlord is so strict about holes in the plaster. He kept my entire security deposit at my last place because of a few thumbtack holes from where I hung up a calendar. It was a really nice calendar, too, with pictures of old lighthouses from the New England coast. My sister got it for me for Christmas. She went to Maine a few years ago and brought back all these blueberry jams and syrups. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, the sleep is just really fragmented. I'm up at 3 or 4 AM and then I'm just awake until I have to get up for my shift.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... I wouldn't say I've actually made a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But, yeah, I do catch myself just wishing I wouldn't wake up, you know? Or thinking it would just be so much easier if I disappeared. Just vanished. 

Like those magic tricks on TV. I used to watch this guy on public access when I was a kid, he always wore this shiny purple cape. He wasn't very good, honestly. You could always see where he hid the dove in his sleeve. But he did this one trick with a disappearing cabinet that was actually pretty impressive. My dad bought me a magic kit for my tenth birthday because I liked that show so much. It came with these plastic cups and little foam balls, and a fake thumb tip. The thumb tip was way too big for me, though, it looked ridiculous. I tried to show my cousin a trick at Thanksgiving that year, but the foam ball just fell right out of my pocket onto the carpet. My aunt laughed so hard she spilled her wine. It was a red wine, a merlot I think, and it got all over my mom's good lace tablecloth. She tried everything to get the stain out—club soda, white vinegar, some expensive spray she bought from a infomercial. Nothing worked. She ended up having to dye the whole tablecloth this weird shade of maroon to cover it up. We used it for Christmas dinners after that, but it always smelled faintly of vinegar. 

But yeah, I don't have a plan to kill myself. Just those thoughts of... not being here anymore.

I'm not scared of choking or throwing up or anything. I just... I don't know, the thought of food just doesn't do anything for me. It's like my stomach is just turned off. Which is weird, because I used to love cooking. I had this whole routine on Sundays where I'd meal prep for the week. I'd make this huge batch of chili, you know? With the diced tomatoes and the kidney beans. I'd always get the store-brand beans because honestly, there's no difference between those and the name brand. It's all a scam anyway. My aunt Linda used to work at a canning factory in Ohio back in the 90s, and she said they literally just put different labels on the exact same cans. She had all these crazy stories about the factory line. One time, a bird got into the warehouse and they had to shut down the whole operation for three hours just to get it out. They ended up having to call animal control because the bird just kept flying up into the rafters. But yeah, no, I'm not afraid of getting sick from eating. I just don't have the urge to do it.\n\nDoctor: Did your reduced appetite begin before you started feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, that's the thing, it's hard to remember exactly when it started. I think the appetite thing might have actually been the first thing I noticed, before the really heavy mood set in. Like I said before, that's what really pushed me to come in, because my eating is just so off. I'm barely getting down coffee and a granola bar before my shift, and my scrubs are just hanging on me. \n\nAnd I really hate when my scrubs don't fit right. It's a safety hazard, honestly. If you're helping transfer a patient from a bed to a wheelchair and you've got all this extra fabric bunching up, it can catch on the armrest. That actually happened to this guy I used to work with, Greg. He was a good guy, but he always wore his scrubs way too baggy. One time he caught his pocket on the brake handle of a transport chair and ripped the whole side of his pants open right in the middle of the hallway. He had to wear a hospital gown tied around his waist like a skirt for the rest of his shift until his wife could bring him a new pair. His wife was a piece of work, too. She showed up an hour later with his pants, but she brought him a large iced coffee from Dunkin' and didn't even bring him a donut or anything. Who goes to Dunkin' and doesn't get a donut? I think it was before the depression really hit, because I remember noticing my clothes were loose before I started feeling so down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling persistently depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, the steady depression... the real loss of interest in everything... that settled in a bit later. I'd say it's been with me for roughly the past year and four months. It's just been this constant, heavy cloud. Around the same time, actually, they changed the parking situation at work. It used to be that support staff could park in the B lot, which is right by the employee entrance. It was so convenient, especially in the winter when the wind comes off the lake. But then they decided B lot was only for physicians and upper management, so they bumped us all out to the overflow lot by the old abandoned strip mall. \n\nIt's a ten-minute walk now, minimum. And you know, they run a shuttle bus, but it's completely unreliable. The driver, this guy named Stan, always takes his breaks at the absolute worst times. I swear he waits until shift change to go sit in the breakroom and eat his egg salad sandwiches. And let me tell you, you do not want to be on that shuttle right after Stan has been eating egg salad. The ventilation on those little buses is terrible. They bought them used from a church in the suburbs, and I don't think they've ever serviced the AC. My neighbor actually bought a used church van once to convert into a camper, but he gave up halfway through because he said he couldn't get the smell of stale incense out of the upholstery. He ended up selling it on Craigslist to some college kids who were starting a band. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about a year and four months of just feeling down all the time.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost since your appetite started declining?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds. Like I was saying a minute ago, that's really what brought me in, the fact that my eating has gotten so off. I'm definitely not trying to lose weight, but I'm just living on that morning coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are just hanging loose on me now. \n\nI haven't actually stepped on a scale to check the number because the one in my bathroom broke a while back. It was one of those sleek glass ones, you know? I accidentally dropped a glass bottle of aftershave on it and the whole scale just shattered. It was that tempered glass, so it broke into a million little square pieces instead of sharp shards, which I guess is supposed to be safer, but let me tell you, it is an absolute nightmare to clean up. I was finding little glass cubes in the grout lines of my bathroom tile for weeks. I had to get out the hose attachment on my vacuum to get the pieces out of the corners, but my vacuum is this ancient Hoover that sounds like a jet engine taking off. My downstairs neighbor actually banged on the ceiling with a broom handle because I was vacuuming at like nine at night. He's so touchy about noise. He works second shift at the bottling plant across town, so his schedule is completely opposite of mine. I try to be considerate, but sometimes you just have to vacuum, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I don't know the exact weight. Just that I'm shrinking out of my work clothes without even trying to.\n\nDoctor: Before you began feeling depressed, were you otherwise functioning normally but still consistently had little interest in eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say I was functioning okay back then, just going to work and doing my thing, but the appetite stuff was definitely there. Like I mentioned, that's really what brings me in now, because my eating has just gotten so off. Some days I barely get down coffee and a granola bar before a shift, and my scrubs have been hanging loose even though I’m not trying to lose weight at all. \n\nActually, speaking of granola bars, they stopped stocking the good ones in the breakroom vending machine. They used to have those chewy peanut butter chocolate chip ones, the ones in the green wrapper? They were perfect if you just needed something quick on the go. But the vending machine guy—I think his name is Carl, he always wears these really loud Hawaiian shirts—he swapped them out for these rock-hard organic oat bars that taste like literal cardboard. I tried one once and I swear I almost chipped a tooth. I don't know who buys them. Probably the dietary aides, they're always going on about macros and fiber. One of them, Jessica, she brings her lunch in these tiny little color-coded containers every single day. She tried to explain her meal prep system to me once while we were waiting for the service elevator, but she talks so fast I couldn't follow half of it. She also has this really annoying habit of clicking her pen when she talks. Click-click, click-click, the entire elevator ride. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, I was doing alright otherwise, just not really eating much and losing the weight without trying.\n\nDoctor: About how much weight have you lost since your appetite began to decline?\n\nPatient: I know you asked me that a minute ago, and I really wish I could give you an exact number. I just haven't been able to weigh myself since that whole disaster with the shattered glass scale in my bathroom. But like I said, that's the main reason I'm here... my eating is just completely off. I'll have my coffee and maybe force down one of those terrible granola bars before my shift, and that's it for the day. My scrubs are just hanging off me now, and I'm honestly not trying to lose weight at all. \n\nIt's actually really annoying because I had to go to the uniform supply store last week to see if I could get a smaller size. The store is way out on the east side of town, right next to this huge intersection where the traffic lights are always out of sync. You sit there for what feels like ten minutes waiting to turn left. And when I finally got there, the woman working the counter—I think her name tag said Brenda—she was on a personal call the entire time she was ringing me up. She was arguing with her sister about who was supposed to bring the potato salad to their family reunion. I mean, I get it, family stuff is stressful, but she didn't even look at me once. She just scanned the new scrubs, shoved them in a bag, and handed me the receipt while yelling into her phone about how she's not making her famous deviled eggs if her sister is going to be ungrateful. I make a pretty good deviled egg myself, actually. The trick is to use a little bit of sweet pickle relish mixed in with the mustard. \n\nBut yeah, I don't have a number for you. Just that my clothes don't fit and I'm barely eating.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been during this period of feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Well, my sleep has been pretty terrible, honestly. It's just... broken up all night. I lie there in the dark, and my brain just starts replaying everything from work, or I'm straining to listen for every little creak and noise in the building. And then I end up waking up around 3 or 4 in the morning anyway, and I just can't settle back down before my alarm goes off. \n\nIt doesn't help that the streetlamp outside my bedroom window is flickering again. It's this awful orange sodium light, and it just buzzes and blinks. I called the city about it three weeks ago. You have to go through this whole automated menu system where a robot voice keeps asking you to say your street name, but it never understands me. I must have said \"Elmwood Drive\" five times before it finally put me through to a real person. And the guy I talked to sounded like he was eating a sandwich the whole time. He promised they'd send a crew out within 48 hours, but obviously, that never happened. I even tried putting up blackout curtains, but the tension rod I bought from the discount store keeps slipping down. The spring inside it is completely shot. I should probably just drill some actual brackets into the wall, but my landlord is so strict about holes in the plaster. He kept my entire security deposit at my last place because of a few thumbtack holes from where I hung up a calendar. It was a really nice calendar, too, with pictures of old lighthouses from the New England coast. My sister got it for me for Christmas. She went to Maine a few years ago and brought back all these blueberry jams and syrups. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, the sleep is just really fragmented. I'm up at 3 or 4 AM and then I'm just awake until I have to get up for my shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of wanting to die or of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't say I've actually made a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But, yeah, I do catch myself just wishing I wouldn't wake up, you know? Or thinking it would just be so much easier if I disappeared. Just vanished. \n\nLike those magic tricks on TV. I used to watch this guy on public access when I was a kid, he always wore this shiny purple cape. He wasn't very good, honestly. You could always see where he hid the dove in his sleeve. But he did this one trick with a disappearing cabinet that was actually pretty impressive. My dad bought me a magic kit for my tenth birthday because I liked that show so much. It came with these plastic cups and little foam balls, and a fake thumb tip. The thumb tip was way too big for me, though, it looked ridiculous. I tried to show my cousin a trick at Thanksgiving that year, but the foam ball just fell right out of my pocket onto the carpet. My aunt laughed so hard she spilled her wine. It was a red wine, a merlot I think, and it got all over my mom's good lace tablecloth. She tried everything to get the stain out—club soda, white vinegar, some expensive spray she bought from a infomercial. Nothing worked. She ended up having to dye the whole tablecloth this weird shade of maroon to cover it up. We used it for Christmas dinners after that, but it always smelled faintly of vinegar. \n\nBut yeah, I don't have a plan to kill myself. Just those thoughts of... not being here anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
